| LP-CR-001 | 2026-03-13 | Async orchestration, compute-job leasing, and worker fencing | Hardened | Initial review found a correctness risk in `analytics_compute_job` claiming: `lease_pending_jobs(...)` and stale-job reconciliation both selected rows and then mutated them without a row-level claim fence. On PostgreSQL, competing compute workers could observe the same pending or stale row before either transaction committed. | Added explicit PostgreSQL `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED` statement builders for pending-job leasing and stale-job reconciliation in `ComputeJobStore`, and added lower-level unit proofs for both statement shapes while retaining existing lifecycle tests. | Continue this pattern review with worker quiescence, queue observability, and documentation drift around the old background-task runtime description. | Branch `review-ledger-async-orchestration`; files: `app/services/compute_job_store.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_compute_job_store.py`; validation: `python -m pytest tests/unit/services/test_compute_job_store.py -q`, `python -m pytest tests/unit/services/test_compute_executor_worker.py -q`, `python -m ruff check app/services/compute_job_store.py tests/unit/services/test_compute_job_store.py`, `python -m mypy --config-file mypy.ini app/services/compute_job_store.py`. |

| LP-CR-013 | 2026-03-14 | Contribution weight division fencing on zero portfolio capital | Hardened | Reviewing contribution methodology against the engine exposed a real correctness gap: `_calculate_daily_instrument_contributions(...)` documented zero-capital weight division as mapping to `0`, but the implementation only filled `NaN`. When portfolio capital was `0` and instrument capital was non-zero, the division could produce `inf`, contaminating raw and smoothed contribution totals. | Normalized the daily weight series by replacing `+/-inf` with `NaN` before `fillna(0.0)`, ensuring zero-capital portfolio days cannot leak infinite weights or contributions into flat or hierarchical aggregation paths. Added a focused unit test that constructs the zero-portfolio-capital edge case and proves weight and contribution outputs are forced to zero. | Next: continue the methodology-depth review for contribution and attribution docs now that the documented zero-weight behavior is truly implementation-backed. | Files: `engine/contribution.py`, `tests/unit/engine/test_contribution.py`; validation: `python -m pytest tests/unit/engine/test_contribution.py -q`, `python -m ruff check engine/contribution.py tests/unit/engine/test_contribution.py`. |
| LP-CR-014 | 2026-03-14 | Hierarchical contribution period-collapse bug | Hardened | Hierarchical contribution requests were contractually modeled as `results_by_period`, but the implementation aggregated across the full master window once and then attached that aggregate only to the first resolved period. Multi-period hierarchy requests therefore returned incorrect results and silently dropped later periods. | Refactored hierarchical aggregation into a reusable period-slice helper, extracted shared portfolio-period return computation in the contribution service, and changed hierarchy mode to iterate resolved periods the same way as the flat path. Added an integration test that proves `MTD` and `YTD` hierarchy results are both returned with distinct values. | Next: align the contribution methodology docs to state the per-period hierarchy behavior explicitly now that the runtime path is fixed and test-backed. | Files: `app/services/contribution_service.py`, `engine/contribution.py`, `tests/integration/test_contribution_api.py`; validation: `python -m pytest tests/integration/test_contribution_api.py -q`, `python -m ruff check app/services/contribution_service.py engine/contribution.py tests/integration/test_contribution_api.py`. |

| LP-CR-016 | 2026-03-14 | Attribution zero-weight and zero-capital division fencing | Hardened | Attribution instrument preparation still had two non-finite division paths inside the derived `by_instrument` path: instrument `weight_bop` could go infinite when portfolio beginning capital was zero, and grouped portfolio returns could go non-finite when a derived group's summed weight was zero. Both cases would pollute aligned panels and create brittle downstream effect calculations. | Hardened `_prepare_data_from_instruments(...)` by replacing `+/-inf` with `NaN` and then forcing deterministic `0.0` weights/returns on the derived instrument/group rollup path. | LP-CR-023 | 2026-03-14 | Duplicate-submission fencing and async replay correctness | Hardened | The executor-backed submission path still used `merge` semantics at the durable boundary. Reusing a `calculation_id` could silently upsert execution or compute-job rows instead of distinguishing a safe idempotent replay from a conflicting payload drift. That is an operational correctness risk in a banking analytics service because replay, reconciliation, and operator polling all depend on `calculation_id` being a trustworthy execution handle rather than an ambient overwrite key. | Added durable registration outcomes to `ExecutionRegistry` and `ComputeJobStore` so the system now distinguishes `created`, `replay`, and `conflict` at the database boundary. | LP-CR-046 | 2026-03-14 | Lineage worker lease fencing and additive schema upgrade safety | Hardened | The compute executor already used durable claim semantics, but the lineage worker still read naked pending payloads and incremented attempt count separately. Retry and terminal-failure paths now release lease state cleanly so work can be reclaimed safely after failure. Extended lineage schema bootstrap with an additive upgrade path that backfills the new lease columns and index into existing SQLite payload tables, preventing runtime breakage on older metadata stores. Added lower-level proofs for lease exclusivity, expired-lease reclaim, lease release on retry, and additive schema migration, then reran the full suite and OpenAPI gate. | Next: if lineage runtime visibility needs to go deeper later, the next logical step is exposing leased-lineage counts/age directly in runtime-status rather than inferring all backlog pressure from pending-only counts. Added unit and integration coverage proving healthy stores report availability `1` and outages report availability `0` without false queue samples. | Next: continue reviewing observability surfaces for similar false-green behavior, especially where control-plane or metrics code currently converts source failures into default-success data. | Files: `app/services/queue_metrics_service.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_queue_metrics_service.py`, `tests/integration/test_integration_capabilities_api.py`; validation: `python -m pytest tests/unit/services/test_queue_metrics_service.py tests/integration/test_integration_capabilities_api.py -q`, `python -m ruff check app/services/queue_metrics_service.py tests/unit/services/test_queue_metrics_service.py tests/integration/test_integration_capabilities_api.py`, `python -m mypy --config-file mypy.ini app/services/queue_metrics_service.py`. |
| LP-CR-072 | 2026-03-14 | Readiness schema-integrity truthfulness | Hardened | The durability readiness check only proved that the metadata database was reachable. Added a lower-level unit proof that missing required tables now produce `durable_metadata_schema_incomplete` instead of a false-ready status. | Next: continue hardening truth surfaces where the service can currently report healthy availability without proving the owned runtime contract is actually intact. | Files: `app/services/durability_health_service.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_durability_health_service.py`; validation: `python -m pytest tests/unit/services/test_durability_health_service.py -q`, `python -m ruff check app/services/durability_health_service.py tests/unit/services/test_durability_health_service.py`, `python -m mypy --config-file mypy.ini app/services/durability_health_service.py`. |
| LP-CR-073 | 2026-03-14 | Recovery-history unreadable false-green in runtime status | Hardened | The runtime-status recovery section treated an unreadable recovery-history snapshot the same as “no retained history yet” when no freshness threshold was configured. | LP-CR-089 | 2026-03-14 | Stateful replay snapshot-build amplification | Hardened | The stateful returns-series orchestration benchmark regressed above its governed budget even though the core stateful retrieval contracts were still green. Investigation showed replay runs with the same `calculation_id` were rebuilding and hashing upstream snapshot payloads even when those snapshots already existed durably, wasting work on the hottest replay path in the composed benchmark. | Added durable snapshot-id introspection to the execution registry and taught the stateful input service to pre-check existing snapshot identities before rebuilding full snapshot payload fingerprints. Existing portfolio, benchmark, and risk-free snapshot records are now skipped before expensive response hashing and JSON building, while new snapshot records still follow the existing durable write path. Added a lower-level test proving duplicate stateful portfolio retrieval no longer rebuilds snapshots once the calculation already owns them. | Next: if stateful replay becomes an even bigger production path, the next stronger step would be caching or memoizing normalized upstream payloads across the full returns-series service path rather than only skipping duplicate snapshot-materialization work. The benchmark regression caused by duplicate snapshot build overhead is now closed. | Files: `app/services/execution_registry.py`, `app/services/stateful_input_service.py`, `tests/unit/services/test_stateful_input_service.py`, `tests/benchmarks/test_returns_series_orchestration_performance.py`; validation: `python -m pytest tests/unit/services/test_stateful_input_service.py tests/benchmarks/test_returns_series_orchestration_performance.py -q`, `python -m ruff check app/services/execution_registry.py app/services/stateful_input_service.py tests/unit/services/test_stateful_input_service.py tests/benchmarks/test_returns_series_orchestration_performance.py`, `python -m mypy --config-file mypy.ini app/services/execution_registry.py app/services/stateful_input_service.py`. |

Added lower-level tests proving the durable store globals follow runtime DB URL changes and that the readiness contract still reports reachable, unreachable, and schema-incomplete states correctly. | Next: continue the same ownership cleanup pattern on any remaining service modules that still capture durable runtime collaborators too early, but only where it changes real runtime/test isolation semantics rather than producing churn. | LP-CR-101 | 2026-03-14 | Pre-merge benchmark and proxy-type contract stabilization | Hardened | During the pre-merge full CI gate, three correctness/governance gaps showed up that the targeted slice packs had not exercised together: the durable-store proxy signatures were not fully reflected in bootstrap/worker typing, the PostgreSQL queue-plan benchmark still called a refactored statement builder with the old signature, and the composed stateful returns-series characterization budget was tighter than the current coverage-backed merge gate on this environment. | LP-CR-107 | 2026-03-14 | Lineage manifest and durable-record completion timestamp consistency | Hardened | After the durable-store UTC audit, the remaining timestamp-hardening gap was filesystem-backed rather than database-backed. `LineageService.materialize_payload(...)` wrote `manifest.json` from the pre-completion durable record and only then advanced the durable lineage record timestamp via `mark_complete(...)`. That could leave the persisted manifest and the DB-backed lineage API disagreeing about when materialization completed, undermining audit-trail trust. | Changed `LineageMetadataStore.mark_complete(...)` to accept an explicit completion timestamp and made `LineageService.materialize_payload(...)` generate a single completion timestamp that is used for both the durable record and the persisted `manifest.json`. | LP-CR-108 | 2026-03-14 | Lineage manifest structural and consistency validation | Hardened | Even after LP-CR-107, the lineage read path still treated `manifest.json` mostly as an existence check. Added service and integration coverage plus public-doc updates to describe the stronger audit contract. | Next: if we keep deepening lineage truthfulness, the next worthwhile pass is reviewing whether any artifact-download or manifest-reader paths still flatten distinct corruption modes into generic availability errors. | LP-CR-156 | 2026-03-15 | Stateful attribution identity and upstream-lineage truth review | Hardened | Review of the new stateful attribution slice found two material correctness gaps. For non-ITD periods such as `MTD` inside `YTD`, cumulative relative performance must use each side's cumulative-to-date return at the period end, not the current period's active return. Added an integration regression in `tests/integration/test_performance_api.py` that proves the difference on a multi-period `MTD` + `YTD` request: `MTD.arithmetic_relative_return` remains the current month active return while `MTD.cumulative_arithmetic_relative_return` correctly reflects cumulative-to-date active return through month end. | Next: the TWR benchmark contract is now much closer to trustworthy for consumer-facing reporting. This keeps the operator-facing unavailable-source semantics intact while reducing repeated collector plumbing.
